22 10:04:52      get the expansion joints through the podium areas, and

24 10:04:57          Next slide, please.
25 10:05:00          So expansion joints are basically gaps in the
                                       7
 1 10:05:03      concrete slab which allow movement of the slabs due to
 2 10:05:07      temperature changes, moisture changes and load
 3 10:05:09      variations.  The expansion joints are waterproofed to
 4 10:05:14      stop water coming through them, and there has been
 5 10:05:17      a number of details included during this project.  This
 6 10:05:22      is one of the details from the approved waterproofing
 7 10:05:25      shop drawings, which shows one of the expansion joint
 8 10:05:29      details.  There's quite a few varieties of these, but
 9 10:05:32      this is one of the more typical ones.
10 10:05:34          You'll see in the bottom left enlarged diagram
11 10:05:40      the expansion joint is 15mm.  It's typically filled by
12 10:05:43      a filler board.  The filler board does no more than just
13 10:05:47      fill the gap.  And then there's details of how the
14 10:05:52      expansion joint is protected by waterproofing protection
15 10:05:56      board and other build-ups around it.
16 10:06:00          So I'm going to -- if we go to the next slide,
17 10:06:04      please -- look at detail B, which is at the top of that.
18 10:06:08          What you can see -- and I've enlarged the labels in
19 10:06:12      the diagram on the right-hand side -- is that there is
20 10:06:14      a layer of 5mm bituminous membrane.  Above that
21 10:06:21      membrane, there's an additional two layers of Torchseal
22 10:06:25      black membrane.  So there's three layers of
23 10:06:28      waterproofing that run along the top of the expansion
24 10:06:30      joints.  There's a small circle, which is the turbo
25 10:06:35      sealant, and that just basically acts as a bead to lay
                                       8
 1 10:06:40      the waterproofing across.
 2 10:06:41          You'll see that the waterproofing has a slight lap
 3 10:06:45      above that round sealant circle, and that's because the
 4 10:06:49      waterproofing has to have some give in it, so that if
 5 10:06:53      the slabs move away from each other, the waterproofing
 6 10:06:56      is not put under any force; it has the ability to move
 7 10:06:59      in and out quite freely.
 8 10:07:02          Above the waterproofing layers, there's a protection
 9 10:07:05      screed and there's also a protection board.
10 10:07:08          So this is basically how the expansion joint detail
11 10:07:13      was put in the drawings and in the method statement.  If
12 10:07:17      the expansion joint waterproofing had been installed in
13 10:07:20      accordance with this approved detail, it would be
14 10:07:24      watertight and there would be no water leakage through
15 10:07:27      into the basement.
16 10:07:28          So it's agreed, common ground, that the
17 10:07:30      specification section 07100 for waterproofing requires
18 10:07:37      an extended warranty of 10 years for waterproofing, and
19 10:07:40      this warranty was provided by the waterproofing
20 10:07:42      specialist, Al Shirawi.
21 10:07:44          If installed correctly, it's difficult to damage
22 10:07:47      that build-up above the membrane and the membrane
23 10:07:51      itself.  Any damage that did occur is unlikely to
24 10:07:53      penetrate through all the layers of build-up, so three
25 10:07:56      layers of waterproofing membrane, the protection screed,
                                       9
 1 10:07:59      the protection board, and then whatever backfill and
 2 10:08:02      finishes you have on top.
 3 10:08:07          So the membrane is protected by these boards and
 4 10:08:10      layers of finishes.  So if there is any damage to the
 5 10:08:13      waterproofing, it's likely to have occurred at the time
 6 10:08:15      of installation, when CSCEC was still responsible for
 7 10:08:18      the site.
 8 10:08:20          And just so you're aware that the build-up between
 9 10:08:24      the waterproofing and the top of the finishes varies
10 10:08:28      between 30cm and probably about 1m across the site,
11 10:08:34      depending on what the levels are above.  But at all
12 10:08:38      locations you have the protection screed, the protection
13 10:08:40      board, three layers of waterproofing and then
14 10:08:42      the finishes and backfill on top.
15 10:08:45          Next slide, please.
16 10:08:50          So it was the responsibility of the contractor to
17 10:08:52      provide an integrated site-wide waterproofing system,

20 10:09:03      subject to water integrity testing; instead, testing was

                                      12
 1 10:12:14      a defect in that waterproofing which allows water to
 2 10:12:16      pass through it.
 3 10:12:17          If the waterproofing was installed as per the
 4 10:12:21      approved method statement and drawings, there would be
 5 10:12:24      no penetration or holes in the waterproofing and the
 6 10:12:28      water would simply not pass through to the expansion
 7 10:12:31      joints.  So leakages into the basement through the
 8 10:12:35      expansion joints only happen where there are defects in
 9 10:12:37      the waterproofing system.
10 10:12:39          If the expansion joints were constructed to comply
11 10:12:41      with the approved details, extreme weather events would
12 10:12:44      not cause water leakages into the basement through the
13 10:12:50      expansion joints, any more than light rainfall would.

16 10:12:59      penetrate.

 2 10:22:31      division 9 finishes, which would indicate that it's
 3 10:22:37      decorative only.
 4 10:22:39          So the coating should form an impermeable barrier to
 5 10:22:43      water infiltration.  And this is quite typical for
 6 10:22:46      traffic coating because it safeguards traffic's concrete
 7 10:22:46      substrates from deterioration caused by moisture and
 8 10:22:52      chemicals.

10 10:22:56      the execution of the traffic coating, starting with the
11 10:23:00      inspection and preparation of the surface.  The surfaces
12 10:23:04      were to be free of debris and dirt, and the substrates
13 10:23:07      were to be powerfloated to remove all contaminants and

15 10:23:16      coating system.

19 10:33:51          So the original specification for the cladding
20 10:33:53      system was for stone cladding with mechanical fixings.
21 10:33:57      The specification following value-engineering was wall
22 10:34:03      cladding with "Dry or Wet Fixing".  The cladding that is

24 10:34:16          So Laminam is a product name.  They produce ceramic
25 10:34:18      tiles which are designed to be large size but with
                                      27
 1 10:34:21      minimal thickness.  They are described as "ultra-thin,
 2 10:34:25      large sized ceramic surfaces".  The Laminam tiles used
 3 10:34:29      were 3.5mm thick and generally panels of 1m by 3m.  Some
 4 10:34:37      of them were cut to smaller size, but many of the tiles
 5 10:34:41      used and installed on the building are 1m by 3m.
 6 10:34:46          The Laminam was fixed using a tile adhesive,
 7 10:34:50      Laticrete 2542.
 8 10:34:59          Next slide, please.
 9 10:35:00          So there were three main design considerations that
10 10:35:02      were necessary to implement this Laminam and Laticrete
11 10:35:10      adhesive cladding.
12 10:35:11          Suitability for use in Dubai.  So the Laminam
13 10:35:13      guidance for external facades confirms the tiles are
14 10:35:16      suitable for use in a climate like Dubai.
15 10:35:20          The method of fixing the tile.  So the contractor
16 10:35:23      was asked to provide proof and reference of the use of
17 10:35:26      Laticrete 254 on external walls in the UAE climatic
18 10:35:29      conditions, which, as you are aware, can be quite

20 10:35:35      evidence that this was done.

                                      28
 1 10:35:55      bonding ability was to the walls.
 2 10:35:58          And thirdly, the tile size.  So Crossville, who

 4 10:36:08      panels, which are the 1m by 3m, for ground floor
 5 10:36:14      installations is acceptable, but for higher
 6 10:36:16      installations the tile size should be limited unless
 7 10:36:19      the engineer and local code approval is given.

16 10:36:50      cladding system.

21 10:39:44          Next slide, please.
22 10:39:46          So what is debonding?  Debonding refers to where
23 10:39:53      Laminam tiles are no longer bonded to the substrate by
24 10:39:56      the Laticrete adhesive.  I've included a photograph on
25 10:39:59      the right-hand side which shows an example of a Laminam
                                      31
 1 10:40:02      slab where you can see one side appeared to be bonded,
 2 10:40:07      but a good third across one corner has debonded.
 3 10:40:11          The debonding of the Laminam tiles may, in part, be
 4 10:40:15      due to the installation in excessively hot and humid
 5 10:40:19      conditions typical of Dubai during the summer months.
 6 10:40:23      These hot and humid conditions would compromise the
 7 10:40:27      initial bond development and subsequent curing of the
 8 10:40:30      adhesive, which would result in hollow tiles.  And
 9 10:40:34      hollow tiles are tiles which have areas which are not
10 10:40:38      properly fixed to the building by adhesive.
11 10:40:41          The installation of Laminam using a wet-fix method
12 10:40:44      is so sensitive to unevenness that defects could easily
13 10:40:49      arise from poor workmanship in the construction of
14 10:40:51      the substrate.  The Laticrete is applied across the back
15 10:40:55      of the tile and to the substrate, and it should fully
16 10:41:01      connect between the tile and the substrate.  If there's
17 10:41:06      any unevenness, it might be that the Laticrete hasn't
18 10:41:11      fully attached to the tile across the full surface and
19 10:41:15      across the full substrate.
20 10:41:17          Next slide, please.

                                      46
 1 11:01:56      defects and which party is responsible for them.
 2 11:02:00          The Laminam.  The original design was changed by
 3 11:02:04      value-engineering.  The interesting point here is that
 4 11:02:06      it was the instigation by DAR and DIEZA to reduce the
 5 11:02:14      large amount of money that was for the stone cladding.
 6 11:02:17      And this was carried out in 2017, and in fact it was
 7 11:02:25      a recommendation by DAR to move to this.  And there were
 8 11:02:36      IFC drawings produced in May of that year, and those
 9 11:02:40      drawings showed -- the format of the tiles on the
10 11:02:43      buildings showed that they were to be mechanically
11 11:02:46      fixed.
12 11:02:48          However -- so that's the original design, was
13 11:02:54      mechanically fixed stone cladding.  The
14 11:02:56      value-engineering changed that cladding, to reduce the
15 11:02:59      cost, to a thin Italian tile, and these were fixed using
16 11:03:03      adhesive mortar.
17 11:03:06          The IFC drawings showed the mechanically fixed.
18 11:03:09      The adhesive mortar, the Laticrete, was really created
19 11:03:17      by DAR, not by China State.  And those are inferior to
20 11:03:24      stone cladding because they're a more difficult
21 11:03:28      installation; they're a less consistent finish because
22 11:03:32      of the difficulty in ensuring uniformity, being so thin;
23 11:03:37      and they have a lower durability and greater
24 11:03:40      susceptibility to damage, which I've seen -- that's
25 11:03:43      mechanical damage -- and they've got higher long-term
                                      47
 1 11:03:46      maintenance requirements.
 2 11:03:48          So what should DAR have done at the time of devising
 3 11:03:52      this alternative cladding?  They should have advised
 4 11:03:56      DIEZA of the key risks with the change, including the
 5 11:03:58      increased likelihood of inconsistent appearance and
 6 11:04:01      colour variation, with greater frequency of damage and
 7 11:04:07      replacement, increased facilities management and
 8 11:04:09      lifecycle maintenance costs, and incorporated mitigation
 9 11:04:14      measures, including smaller areas of tiles, thicker
10 11:04:17      tiles, mechanical fixings and protective capping
11 11:04:20      details, and to procure more spare tiles, because that's
12 11:04:23      what would be needed.
13 11:04:24          Interestingly, the 3.35mm tiles cannot be fixed
14 11:04:28      sensibly by mechanical fixings.  So what was on the
15 11:04:33      drawing, there was no thickness on the IFC drawings;
16 11:04:36      it was left open to conjecture what might be done.  And
17 11:04:40      I don't think it was thought through by DAR.
18 11:04:43          The Laminam installation was compliant with
19 11:04:47      the specification, and DAR issued letters to DIEZA
20 11:04:51      demonstrating compliance by China State with that
21 11:04:56      specification on many occasions.  On 13 November,

23 11:05:03      25 November 2019; 26 September 2022; and one on

25 11:05:17          "... once again confirm ... the Laminam [was]
                                      48
 1 11:05:19      installed in accordance with the contract document
 2 11:05:21      and ... within the tolerances/standard limits ..."

 4 11:05:28          There have been issues, we know; you've seen what
 5 11:05:32      Cathy Joshi has shown latterly.  There's general wear
 6 11:05:36      and tear, there's localised damage, and discrete and
 7 11:05:40      localised partial debonding.
 8 11:05:43          The revised details: when this started occurring,
 9 11:05:45      China State proposed revised detailing, including
10 11:05:47      mechanical fixings and capping, and this proposal was
11 11:05:51      implemented in specific locations.

13 11:05:55      rectification works were completed during 2019 and 2020,
14 11:05:58      and DAR recommended a maintenance contract with regular
15 11:06:04      inspections.

17 11:06:08      disproportionate.  There are very limited incidents of
18 11:06:12      tiles recorded as falling; two, from my investigation of
19 11:06:16      the evidence.  There's no evidence of progressive
20 11:06:19      systematic failure.  And the issues can be managed
21 11:06:24      through a programme of inspection, repair and
22 11:06:26      replacement.  Full replacement is technically,
23 11:06:29      economically and environmentally unjustifiable.
24 11:06:32          The recommended maintenance approach that I suggest
25 11:06:36      is: twice-yearly facade inspections; targeted repair or
                                      49
 1 11:06:41      replacement of damaged or partially debonded tiles; and
 2 11:06:44      that drone inspections are used for upper-level
 3 11:06:48      inspections.  That's not to repair the tiles; that's
 4 11:06:51      just to inspect them.  They would have to be repaired by
 5 11:06:54      cherry-picker or staging.
 6 11:06:56          To conclude, the Laminam tiles are inferior to stone
 7 11:07:00      cladding.  There are ongoing risks and maintenance costs
 8 11:07:03      that should have been fully considered during the
 9 11:07:05      value-engineering exercise and explained by DAR to DIEZA
10 11:07:09      before they went down that route.
11 11:07:13          DAR issued letters to DIEZA demonstrating compliance

16 11:07:27      And the issues can be managed through a programme of
17 11:07:30      regular maintenance and inspection.  Full replacement is
18 11:07:34      technically, economically and environmentally
19 11:07:37      unjustifiable.

13 16:16:55      that hopefully any fluids on it can be equally cleaned.
14 16:17:00          If you leave concrete as itself, it's porous.  Stuff
15 16:17:05      will soak into it; not just water, oil.  It will become
16 16:17:12      rather more of a mess than it is now, but the mess is
17 16:17:15      causing by the traffic, droppings from the vehicles.
18 16:17:20          But it's a coating to protect it.
